                              3.   Experimental Evidence

     Almost all researchers who believe the conventional theory have orthodoxically
been looking for experimental evidence such as the heat and the fusion products of
neutrons, tritiums and heliums (3-6). However, cold fusion is based on the different
reactions, therefore other experimental evidences should be researched. The author
has been searching for such experimental evidencce by using a special technique with
nuclear emulsions. Unusual particles and phenomena were obtained, which clearly indi­
cate that the mechanisms mentioned in the preceding section actually take place. In
this section, the experimental evidence that was obtained mainly by the author's experi­
ments is described.
                                   3. 1   Experiments

     Many cold fusion experiments using the electrolysis method were carried out by the
author (10, 13-22, 27, 28). Ordinary and heavy waters were used as electrolyte solu­
tions mainly mixed with potassium carbonate and sodium chloride. Metals of pal­
ladium, nickel, titanium and copper were often used as the cathode and everytime plati­
num was used as the anode, respectively. Two kinds of cylindrical glass cells were
used for the anodes of rod and foil metals. The anode of the rod metal that were
surrounded by the helical cathode of platinum was totally inserted in the electrolyte
solution. Nuclear emulsions (100 µm thick MA-7B made by Fuji Film Inc.) were
often used to observe emitted particles. The nuclear emulsions were set outside the
glass cell. On the other hand, for foil method, one of the foil surfaces was contacted
with the electrolyte solution and the other with nuclear emulsions in the atmosphere.
Here emitted particles from the metal foil can directly be recorded on the nuclear emul­
sions. Furthermore, optical and scanning electron microscopes (SEM) with energy
dispersive X-ray spectroscopy (EDX) were mainly used to analyze the metal structure
and fusion products.

                               3. 2   Hydrogen -Clusters

     The hypothesis that the hydrogens form the clusters in the small space to induce
the hydrogen-catalyzed fusion reactions is the fundamental consideration of the Nattoh
model. Much experimental evidence clearly indicated that the hydrogen-clusters are
really formed and that the fusion reactions really take place in the compressed clusters.
The first is the break up of the itonic hydrogen-clusters. The hydrogen-clusters that
are extremely compressed are covered up by the itons. They are further compressed
to form heavy multiple-neutrons by the completely symmetrical compression. However




                                          -33-
212                               Takaaki MATSUMOTO


if it is not complete, for example, one or more fusion reactions precede in the cluster,
the hydrogen-cluster might break up. Much experimental evidence suggesting the
break up of the itonic hydrogen-clusters were recorded on copper metals during the
discharging experiments with pin cathodes (28). Then among the broken itonic hydro­
gens, there were observed evidences of the fusion processes. The number of the hydro­
gens contained in the cluster depends on the conditions such as applied voltage and
current. For high voltage, such as around 100 V, the number of the contained hydro­
gens might be more than millions. Traces of the broken hydrogen-clusters containing
many hydrogens were observed on the copper plate.
     Secondly, the itonic meshes covering the hydrogen-cluster faded while passing
through the material so that the linkages of the individual hydrogens become weaken
and finally scatter. This is a reverse process of the compression of the hydrogen-clus­
ter. Those evidences were also observed on the back surface of the nickel thin foil,
referred to the "hydrogen-frost"Cl3). They seem to have some structure like solidified
vapor.
                            3. 3 Primary Fusion Products

    The most probable reactions during the cold fusion are shown by Eqs. (4) to (8)
for heavy water and Eqs. (26) to (30) for ordinary water. For the latter, the char­
ged particles of the electrons, protons and deuteriums that are primary fusion products
were clearly observed on the first nuclear emulsion that was contacted with the nickel
cathode (13). And the di-neutrons were also measured on the nuclear emulsions and
on the metal surface (13, 28). The single neutrons also underwent the gravity decays
because they were covered with the itonic mesh. Those traces were separately obser­
ved on the metal surface during the one-point discharge experiments (28). For heavy
water, on the other hand, no charged particles of the primary products were observed
on the nuclear emulsions, because a thin protection sheet was located over the first
nuclear emulsion. But Miles et al. measured a lot of 4 He instad of 3 He by the chemi­
cal method (8), as predicted by the Nattoh model. And the di-and quad-neutrons
were successfully observed on the nulcear emulsions (A micro-explosion that was
caused by the gravity decay of the quad-neutron was first observed on August 25 in
1990 on a nuclear emulsion that was located outside of the glass cell in the experiment
of palladium rod/heavy water mixed with sodium chloride, shown in Fig. 1 (21)).
They are covered up with the itonic meshes that are broken at the explosion of the
inner multiple-neutrons. Remarkable traces indicating the explosions of the quad-neu­
trons associated with the break up of the itonic meshes were found (20). Furthermore
a trace indicating that four fusion reactions simultaneously occur in the hydrogen-clus­
ter was also observed (18).
                              3. 4 New Particle "Itons"

    The Nattoh model predicts the production of the new particle "itons" during the
hydrogen-catalyzed fusion reactions. The itons are considered to consist of electrons,
positrons and neutrinos. Since more electrons than positrons are involved, the itons
are negatively charged. When the itons are produced during the compression process




                                        -34-
                                Mechanisms of Cold Fusion :                           213


of the hydrogen-cluster, they are emitted to cover up the nuclei of the product parti­
cles. Figure 3 in Ref. 20 clearly shows the break ups of the itonic products, inside
that the quad-neutron underwent the gravity decay. While the iton passes through the
material, the electrons of the iton are torn off by a de-ionization process and the iton
finally decays to a pair of an electron and a positron. The sequential break ups of the
network of the itonic meshes were successfully observed on the nuclear emulsions,
shown in Fig. 4 in Ref. 20. And the pair productions due the itons were also recorded
on the nuclear emulsions (14).

                                3. 5 Chain-Reactions

     The Nattoh model predicts that the chain-reactions of the hydrogens can be
maintained to predominately produce the excess heat for the Pons-Fleischmann type
experiment. And the critical equation for the indirect mode was obtained, Eqs. (ll)
to (13). It is difficult to obtain experimental evidence that directly prove the occur­
rence of the chain-reactions in metal. However there is data suggesting it. First, a
critical value seems to exist for the atomic ratio of D/Pd, that needs to be over 0.85 in
average to ignite the burning (6). Under that value, cold fusion cannot be induced at
all. Second, the time behavior of the power can be well presented by Eq. (14) : it
approaches exponentially to the constant level that can be determined by the surface
condition.
                           3. 6 Heavy Element Production

     The EDX analyses with SEM indicated that there are heavy elements distributed in
the grain-shaped defects (21). They consist of wide and continuous nuclei ranging
from sodium to zinc (the EDX system is not available to lighter elements than sodium)
so that they can be assigned to be produced by cold fusion processes. The Nattoh
model predicts that the many-body fusion reactions can easily take place in the com­
pressed hydrogen-clusters to produce the heavy elements. During the explosive cold
fusion, the burning was violent so that those heavier elements could be easily produced.
The neutron-induced radioactive method found that the isotope of 1151n was produced
(29). This was caused by the nuclear transmutation of the host metal Pd, shown in
Eq. (18). On the other hand, Bush reported that Ca was produced from K during the
electrolysis of ordinary water with thin nickel foil, as shown in Eq. (16) (12). Ohmori
et al. reported the production of Fe on the surface of Au electrode, as shown in Eq.
(17) (30). Furthermore Kucherov et al. reported the production of 23 nuclei ranging
from He to Ru (31). Here the many-body fission reaction should be involved.
                               3. 7 Multiple-Neutrons

     The Nattoh inodel predicts the production of the multiple-neutrons such as di- and
quad-neutrons during the hydrogen-catalyzed fusion reactions. The di-and quad-neu­
trons are the primary products of cold fusion with ordinary and heavy water, respec­
tively. Those traces were observed on the nuclear emulsions, described in Sec. 3.3.
Since many hydrogens are involved in the hydrogen-clusters, the other branches for the
production of heavy multiple-neutrons might be possible. Although extremely heavy




                                         -35-
214                                Takaaki MATSUMOTO


mutiple-neutrons can be trasformed to the tiny blackholes, as described in Sec. 3.9,
intermediate multiple-neutrons such as 6n and 8n might be produced. They also are
compressed by the itonic meshes to explode. Traces suggesting those explosions were
found on the nuclear emulsions (17).
                                 3. 8 Defects in Metal

    Metals that were used for the cold fusion experiments were analyzed by SEM (27).
Strange defects were observed in the grain-boundaries in the metals : spot and grain­
shaped defects. The spot defects suggest one-dimensional burning of the cold fusion
and by those sequential reactions, the grain-shaped defects are formed. They are
arranged in the grain-boundaries so that these defects verify that as the Nattoh model
predicts, the cold fusion reactions occur not in the lattice but in the grain-boundaries in
that the hydrogens can be trapped to form the hydrogen-clusters.
                                3. 9 Many-Body Fission

     The multiple-neutrons such as di- and quad-neutrons that are predominately emit­
ted during cold fusion are expected to interact with nuclei in the surrounding materials
to induce nuclear reactions. The compound nuclei caused by the multiple-neutrons
might have a higher exciting energy than by the ordinary neutron. Therefore nuclear
fission reactions with many fragments might be possible.
     Stars indicating those reactions were obtained in an experiment with palladium foil
and heavy water (19). The traces were recorded on the nuclear emulsions located
outside the cell. They can be clearly distinguished from the stars caused by the cosmic
rays. The stars suggest that the multiple-neutrons induced the many-body fission reac­
tions with heavy nuclei, probably silver, in the nuclear emulsions. The number of the
fragments was around seven or eight, and they might somewhat contain radioactive
nuclei. The many-body fission reaction should more frequently take place in the cell
and somewhat contribute to the heat production and the nuclear transmutation.

                          3. 10 Tiny Black and White Holes

     When the nuclei of the host metal and the electrolyte are compresssed in the hydro­
gen-cluster, more than 100 neutron nucleus can be produced that might be transformed
into a tiny black hole by the gravity decay. The extraordinary trace shown in Fig. 4 in
Ref. 18 indicates that a tiny black hole is produced and rolling in the neighboring mate­
rials. Due to the light mass, the tiny black hole evaporates instantaneously, as shown
in Fig. 1 in Ref. 18.
     The black hole is connected with a white hole from which the absorbed materials
can be sprouted out. Many tiny white holes were observed in experiments with low
hydrogen permeability such as nickel (13). Figures in Ref. 13 show those tiny white
holes, in that the broken materials are sprouting from the top of the cone. Remark­
ably, Fig. 7 (i) in Ref. 13 shows both of the evaporation of the black hole and the
sprouting of the materials from the white hole. They might have been divided while
passing through a warm hole that connects the black and white holes.
     It is now uncertain what materials can be sprouted from the white hole. Since the




                                         -36-
                               Mechanisms of Cold Fusion:                            215


nuclei are destroyed by the gravity decays and the broken materials pass through the
worm hole of the order of 10- cm so that they might at least be non-baryons, and
                               33



consist of the tiny particles such as superstrings. The linked materials can somewhat
be seen in the exit of the white holes (13). Furthermore traces suggesting that some
heavy particles such as X-particle might have been emitted were observed on the
nuclear emulsions (13, 20). Those pictures were published in a book (32).

                                    3. 11 Miscellaneous

     Several extraordinary phenomena were found during cold fusion. The first is
strange microsparks that were observed on the metal surface, such as titanium during
the AC electrolysis experiments (22). They can be distinguished from the conventional
electrical sparks. The microsparks were found to be related to the tiny black or white
holes by setting the nuclear emulsions. Since the electrical charging was continuously
employed, the microsparks were shining near the surface like twinkling stars. How­
ever during other experiments with the shot charging (33), the microsparks flighted 2
or 3 cm in the solution or air, like ball lightening, and finally stopped to explode.
Furthermore strange traces of coupled rings that were suggested to be caused by the
tiny ball-lightning were found on nuclear emulsions. They were symmetrically record­
ed on the first and second nuclear emulsions. This means that the tiny ball-lightning
hopped up and down in a small space between the first and second nuclear emulsions.
These strange behaviors can be well explained by the characteristics of the itonic
hydrogen-clusters.
     The second is the extraordinary conductivity that were recently reported by McKu­
bre et al. (23). While they were measuring the electrical resistence to know the
amount of hydrogens absorbed in a metal, the resistence sharply dropped. According
to the Nattoh model, hydrogen-clusters that are locally compressed in small spaces in a
metal such as grain-boundaries become the itonic state, that are negatively charged due
to the surplus electrons and well conductive. As the hydrogen loading ratio increases,
the hydrogen-clusters become densely distributed and interconnects each other. Finally
the rod of the metal hydride becomes wholely conductive.
     The third is interference phenomena that were also observed on the nuclear emul­
sions located outside the cell (16). The gravitational and anti-gravitational waves
were suggested for them. They should have a weak intensity but might microscopi­
cally interact with the material of the nuclear emulsions. But these should be
examined in more detail.

                                      4. Discussions

    In the preceding sections, the mechanisms of the cold fusion were explained by the
Nattoh model and the experimental results that verify the explanations were shown.
Here the controversial issues of the cold fusion and the other important subjects are
discussed.

                              4. 1 Controversial Issues

    The anomalies of the cold fusion that are controversial issues can well be explained




                                          -37-
216                               Takaaki MATSUMOTO


by the N attoh model.
     (a) The primary controversy is the reproducibility of the excess heat. After cold
fusion was published, many researchers tried to replicate the experiments. However
very few researchers succeeded. Alternatively, even in same laboratories, results de­
pended on experimental conditions, especially used palladium rods. These are caused
by the characteristics of the chain-reactions of hydrogens that is a predominate source
of the excess heat for metals such as palladium with the high hydrogen permeability.
The hydrogen concentration should be increased such that the critical condition of Eqs.
(11) to (13) is maintained. However during the charging of hydrogens into palladium,
tiny cracks or pits are produced. The absorbed hydrogens rapidly leak from these
place. Then the critical condition cannot be satisfied. Alloys of palladium with about
10% silver that protects them from cracking can improve the reproducibility of the
excess heat production (6).
     On the other hand, with metals such as nickel with the low hydrogen permeability,
the main source of the excess heat is the surface reaction. Since the hydrogen-clusters
are mainly compressed by the overpotential, the excess heat can be produced as soon
as the charging starts. The reproducibility can be expected to be almost 100%. In the
case of the Mills type experiment, the chain-reactions of hydrogens contribute less to
the heat production than in the Pons-Fleischmann type experiment, because they occur
only on the surface. The other reactions such as the nuclear transmutation given by
Eqs. (16) and Cl 7) and the gravity decay reactions contribute more to the heat pro­
duction. The contribution ratio of the reactions depends on the experimental condition.
The nuclear transmutation of the Ca or Fe production is predominate in the low volt­
age or current density. As the voltage or current density increases, the contribution by
the reactions shown in the lower part in Fig. 1 becomes more.
     (b) The inconsistency between the production rates of the neutrons and the excess
heat described in (b) in Sec. 1, are often controversial for heavy water. This can well
be explained by the hydrogen-catalyzed fusion reactions that emit no neutrons. For
palladium, the main source of the excess heat is the chain-reactions of the hydrogen­
catalyzed fusion reactions where the new particle itons are emitted, taking away about
20 MeV from the excited •He nucleus for heavy water. The channel of the 2.5 MeV
neutron emission can not be opened. Furthermore, the multiple neutron nuclei that are
produced in the reactions are covered up with the itonic meshes so that the individual
neutrons can not be scatted out. However, the conventional D-D fusion reaction of Eq.
(1) that emitts 2.5 MeV neutron takes place as a secondary process, about 106 times
less than the primary hydrogen-catalyzed fusion reactions. Therefore the production
rate of the 2.5 MeV neutrons is at least 106 times less than that of the excess heat.
     A comment should be added for the itonic multiple neutrons. Especially the di­
and quad-neutrons that are emitted during the hydrogen-catalyzed fusion reactions
have the itonic mesh with heavy negative charges. They are sometimes confused with
the conventional neutrons or alpha particles (6). Those multiple neutrons can be
detected by the conventional neutron detector such as scintillators and ionization cham­
bers. Here, the multiple neutrons generate by the gravity decay a peak signal with a
very narrow width so it could be easily distinguished from the neutron signal with care-




                                        -38-
                                 Mechanisms of Cold Fusion :                             217


ful attension. Alternatively, they also generate similar signals to alpha particles in the
charged particle detectors such as surface barrier detectors. The multiple neutrons can
escape out of the cell or filters that can sufficiently stop the alpha particles.
     (c) The inconsistency between the production rates of the tritiums and neutrons,
described in (c) in Sec. 1, is an important issue for heavy water. The activity of the
tritiums is observed 10• to 108 times higher than that calculated from the neutron pro­
duction rate. According to the Nattoh model, •H is expected to be produced during the
hydrogen-catalyzed fusion reactions for heavy water, as shown by Eq. (5). This iso­
tope undergoes the beta-decay such that the activity might be confused with that of the
tritium. Although the lifetime of the 4 H isotope is about 10- 11 sec, it could be stable
for a moment since it is covered up with the itonic mesh. Afetr the mesh fades, the
beta decay instantaneously occurrs. So far the isotope of •H has not yet been observed
in the author's experiments with nuclear emulsions. An assembly of the nuclear emul­
sions was always covered by a thin protection paper that might have prevented the
itonic •H from escaping. However, other researchers reported extraordinary results
that suggest the production of •H. An element with a mass between 4.0026 amu of •He
and 4.0282 amu of D2 was observed (34).
     (d) As described in Sec. 1, a large amount of •He instead of 3 He was reported by
Miles et al. (8). The production of •He would be posssible by the conventional fusion
reaction shown by Eq. (3). If so, however, the intense high energy gamma rays
should be associated. Rather, this fact can easily be explained by the Nattoh model.
The reaction shown by Eq. (4), that is the most probable branch during the hydrogen­
catalyzed fusion reactions, shows the production of 'He. The di-neutrons, associated
here, are covered by the itonic mesh and quickly undergo the gravity decays. If a
neutron detector, such as a liquid scintillator, is located sufficiently near the metal, the
survival di-neutrons might be counted. Almost all of the neutron detectors are located
in large moderators so that very few signals are produced by the di-neutrons.
Furthermore, 'He can be produced by the beta-decay of •H, as shown by Eq. (6).
     (e) The complicated spectra of the charged particles, that cannot be explained by
the conventional D-D fusion reactions were reported by a thin foil experiment (9).
The spectra were explained by introducing the new particle, iton (26) : therefore, the
multiple neutrons such as the di-and quad-neutrons would be emitted as bare particles.
It was later found that they are covered up by the itonic mesh with a heavy charge
(20). They might be confused with the conventional charged particles such as the
deuteriums and heliums in the thin foil experiment. If these factors are enclosed, the
agreement between the experiment and calculation will be better.
     (f) It is important that ordinary water is available to cold fusion. Despite that the
feasibility cannot be expected by the conventional theory at all, the extraordinary sig­
nals indicating cold fusion were first obtained with ordinary water by the anthor (10).
And Mills et al. reported the excess heat production with ordinary water by using a
thin nickel foil (ll). The N attoh model equally predicts the feasibility of cold fusion
with ordinary water, as well as with heavy water. The excess heat production with
ordinary water with the thin nickel foil is somewhat less than with heavy water in the
palladium rod, because the chain-reaction of hydrogens is limitted on the surface in the




                                          -39-
218                                Takaaki MATSUMOTO

former case. However, by using the surface reactions, where the nuclear transmuta­
tion and the gravity decay reactions contribute to the heat production, many experimen­
tal data with the heat production were reported so far (3-6, 30). Many traces that
indicate the gravity decays were observed on the nuclear emulsions (13).
    (g) Other strange phenomena observed during cold fusion can be partially ex­
plained by the Nattoh model. For example the microsparks that were observed on the
metal surface can be explained by the formation of the itonic grain (22).

                      4. 2 Comparison with the Fission Reactor

     Let us briefly compare cold fusion with the fission reactor. For the fission reactor,
the primary process is the neutron-induced fission reactions of uranium or plutonium
and the power is maintained by the neutron chain-reactions. Additionally there are
various reactions associated with the neutron chain-reactions: beta decays of the fission
products, Cherenkov lights by the beta decay electrons, gamma rays by (n, gamma)
reactions and so on. Cold fusion resembles the fission reactor, i.e., the fundamental
reactions of cold fusion are the chain-reactions of the hydrogen-catalyzed fusion reac­
tions that are associated with various reactions. The fuel of cold fusion is the hydro­
gen-clusters and the hydrogens themselves are the chain-reaction particles.
     The biggest differences between them are the distance over which the chain-reac­
tion particles can fly and the multiplicity by that they are multified per reaction. The
neutrons can fly about 2 m in the fission core, while the hydrogens have very short
ranges in metal, only several hundreds µm. And 2.5 neutrons in average are emitted
per one fission reaction of uranium or plutonium, however many energetic hydrogens
per hydrogen-catalyzed fusion reaction are emitted. This is because the fuel of the
hydrogenclusters consist of many hydrogens, possibly more than millions. The former
fact makes cold fusion burn very locally. And the latter means that the critical condi­
tion can easily be satisfied for cold fusion. Furthermore cold fusion burns more vari­
ously. Although the neutron flux in the nuclear reactor is limited to around 10 14 n/cm2•
sec, the hydrogens in the metal for cold fusion can be densely concentrated. The rates
of the associated reactions during cold fusion are so high that the nuclear transmutation
such as the heavy element production is more accelerated than in the fission reactor.
     It is remarkable that despite of the high production rate of the elements, there are
almostly no radiation. This is due to the formation of the itonic mesh that can take
away excitation energy of the compund nucleus.
                                   4. 3 Applications

    Several applications of the cold fusion technique are promising.
    (a) The energy production is the most important at the present time. There are
three processes that contribute to the heat production : chain-reactions of the hydrogen­
catalyzed fusion reactions, nuclear transmutation and gravity decays of the multiple
neutrons. The first is the main source of the excess heat for the Pons-Fleischmann
type experiment with the palladium rod, and the two Iatters for the Mills et al. type
experiment with the thin nickel foil, respectively. The first that is sustained by the
chain-reaction would be good for the efficiency of the heat production to input electrical




                                         -40-
                                Mechanisms of Cold Fusion :                           219


power but the most difficult for the controllability of the reaction. We should be care
that the nuclear transmutation of heavy elements over Fe contributes to the negative
energy production. Then the efficiency of the heat production could not be so im­
proved, even if the hydrogen concentration or the current density are efficiently enhan­
ced. The transforming efficiency from mass to energy is the highest for the third proc­
ess. For scaling up of power, it is required to achieve the high energy density. Then
the method based on the third process might be superior.
     For cold fusion, burst burning is easier than continuous burning. Burst burning is
suitable to vehicle engines such as automobile, ships and space-crafts. It can be perfor­
med by several conditions : direct mode of the chain-reactions, overcritical condition of
the indirect mode or the surface reactions. The two formers are enhanced in the metal
so that the damage of the materials will have unevitable problems. The last that is
especially based on the gravity decays of the multiple neutrons might be more efficient
to generate the propulsion power.
     (b) Nuclear transmutations also might be possible. There are three ways to
obtain nuclear transmutations: production of the elements by the many-body fusion
reactions, nuclear transmutation from the nuclei of the host metal and the electrolyte
and many-body fission reaction. Unlike in the fission reactor, the transmutation rate
for cold fusion is very high because the hydrogen concentration is extremely higher
than the neutron flux. The elements produced by the first process continuously distrib­
ute from lithium to around iron. The second can produce elements near the host nu­
clei. With the third process, produced elements could be widely distributed.
     (c) Nuclear transmutation might be applied to nuclearly incinerate hazardous
materials such as nuclear materials and radioactive wastes. The first method of the
nuclear incineration would be to use the capturing one or more protons and oxygens as
shown in Eqs. (16) to (17). Cesium-137 or strontium-90 would be contained in an
electrolyte solution and transuranium elements such as pultonium and neptium would be
mixed in electrodes. Simultaneously they could be incinerated by the bombardment
with multiple neutrons such as di-and quad-neutrons. In the former process, the
nuclear transmutation takes place in the itonic state so that no radiation would be
emitted. In the latter process, on the other hand, additional radioactive materials
would be somewhat generated.

                                4. 4 Safety Problems

     When cold fusion is applied in the industrial scale, several safety problems should
be resolved.
     (a) The first is explosions. The explosions were often experienced during the elec­
trolysis cold fusion. They could sometimes be hydrogen gas explosions, that can easily
be avoided with usual care. But even under the normal operation, the preceding explo­
sive cold fusion reactions can easily induce the gas explosions. Here the distance
between the electrodes is an important factor. If it is too short, local and small dis­
charges can induce explosive cold fusions. More attention should be payed to control­
ling the cold fusion reactions as well as the chemical reactions.
     (b) Second, radioactive materials could be produced by the fission reactions like in




                                         -41-
220                               Takaaki MATSUMOTO

the fission reactor. For cold fusion, the many-body fission reactions are induced by the
multiple-neutrons such as the di-and quad-neutrons. Furthermore, the reaction of Eq.
(31) generates tritium with ordinary water. They are secondary reactions so that
those amounts are extremely less than the primary one. But in the industrial applica­
tions, the burn up should be so high that the amount of the radioactive materials is
somewhat of be a problem. Even if so, the problems might significantly be reduced
than in the case of the fission reactor.
     (c) The third is related to the gravity decays. They newly generate tiny black
and white holes that might radiate the gravitational waves and non-baryon products.
These might already exist in the natural environment, since the cold fusion reactions
seem to easily take place anywhere. However, they will be additionally produced with
the industrial scale by the applications of cold fusion. This is similar to the problems
of the radiations or radioactive materials in the fission reactors. The materials them­
selves might not be so hazardous but if those magnitudes are over the limit, they will
become problems. The effects of those strange materials on biological tissues should
be examined in detail.

                             4. 5 Impacts on Other Fields

     As mentioned in Sees. 2 and 3, various significant discoveries were made during
cold fusion: (1) new hydrogen-catalyzed fusion reactions associated with the itons, (2)
heavy elements production by the many-body fusion reactions, (3) gravity decays to
generate the tiny black and white holes and so on. These discoveries in cold fusion
might significantly impact on other fields.
     (a) The iton could open new science and technology for nuclear physics and mate­
rial science. Since energy concerned with nuclear physics and material science is very
different each other, the two fields had no direct connection so far. The iton that
locates significantly near the nucleus can directly interact with the nuclei and transfer
energy of the nuclei to material lattice. The spatial distribution and energy of elec­
trons in the itonic mesh should be investigated in detail.
     (b) Elementary physics might be stimulated, since the gravity decays followed by
the tiny black and white holes can be produced in our laboratories. There seems to
exist the critical mass that distinguishes the light multiple-neutrons such as quad­
neutrons from heavy ones, although the exact value is now unknown. The multiple­
neutrons with light mass undergo the gravity decay to explode with shock waves that
were observed like bubbles on the nuclear emulsions. The bubbles certainly consist of
much smaller particles than quarks. Heavy multiple-neutrons, on the other hand, are
transformed to tiny blackholes that evaporate to emit radiation and particles. The
particles should be much smaller than the particles of the bubbles. Therefore, there
might exist the level for the dimension of the particles. Furthermore the quantum-rela­
tivity theory can be examined in detail by experiments in laboratory. A simple ques­
tion arises: why can the significant reactions such as the gravity decay occur in low
voltage electrolysis around several 10 V, although no high energy accelerators, exixting
or planning, can induce them at all. The answer is the compression effect of hydrogen­
cluster that is essentially caused by many-body interaction. Recently, an accelerator




                                        -42-
                                     Mechanisms of Cold Fusion :                                   221

induced cold fusion reactions by accelerating hydrogen-clusters (35).
     ( c) Extraordinary phenomena were observed in the natural environment so far
(36). Many of them are found to be associated with the electrical discharge, but left
unexplained. However they also might be made clear by similar mechanisms to cold
fusion. For example, tiny ball lightenings were observed during a discharging cold
fusion experiment (33).
     (d) The astrophysics might modify the senario by the discovery of the new fusion
reactions. For example, the solar neutrino problems should be re-examined by induc­
ing the new fusion reactions, because the hydrogen concentration deep in the sun is so
high that new fusion reactions can easily be enhanced. Alternatively, the scenario of
the element production in the origin of the universe should also be modified. We can
remember the events that occur in cold stars faraway in the universe. The prodution
of the elements, multiple-neutrons and tiny black holes in cold fusion would correspond
to the productions of white dewarves, neutron-stars and big black holes in the universe,
respectively. We can say that cold fusion is a small scale simulation of the events that
occur in cold stars faraway in the universe. Now we can reproduce the novels of the
universe in our laboratory. Therefore, the non-baryons of the gravity decay products
might be found during cold fusion, that can be expected to be dark matters.

                                         4. 6 Summaries

    In the preceding sections, the mechanisms of cold fusion have been well explained
by the Nattoh model. Although important things such as the productions of 4 H and non
-baryon particles remain unproven, we have comprehensively but qualitatively under­
stood the extraordinary phenomena associated with cold fusion. It is characteristic
that cold fusion burns in various manners depending on the conditions, because the
hydrogen-cluster envolves many hydrogens. The branching ratios of the hydrogen­
catalyzed fusion reactions are critical factors that are determined by the conditions of
the electrolysis, the microscopic structure of the metals and so on. They will be stud­
ied quantitatively from now, but it seems very laborious because there are many cases.
Furthermore, the study of cold fusion will be progressed towards the high voltage and
current to achieve the higher efficiency of transforming mass to energy. There the
gravity decays instead of the fusion reactions predominate so that new science such as
black and white holes will be fully developed.

                                    MATSUMOTO Takaaki


                     Department of Nuclear Engineering, Hokkaido University

                                             Abstract


        Electro-nucelar reactions which take place in a special state of hydrogen clusters and
were recently discovered during cold fusion study were reviewed in a previous paper(l),
"Mechanisms of Cold Fusion: Comprehensive Explanation by The Nattoh Model" This paper
reviews electro-nuclear collapse which is the most remarkable reaction among the electro­
nuclear reactions. Experimental methods of underwater spark discharges are described A lot
of results of the electro-nuclear collapse are obtained The mechanisms of those products are
explained by The Nattoh Model. Natural phenomena caused by electro-nuclear collapse are
described Furthermore, impacts to other scientific fields are also discussed




       Distributed at ICCF-7 in Vancouver, 1998

   (The 7-th International Conference of Cold Fusion)

       Distributed at ICOPS in Monterey, 1999

   (The International Conference of Plasma Science)

l. Introduction

l.u. What is Elecrtru-Nuclear Collapse(ENC)?
        Nuclear collapses by the gravitational force take place far in the universe after shining

stars consume their fuels. As resulted, black holes which absorb materials could be formed with

very massive stars. Many astrophysicists now believe that a black hole really exists even in our

galaxy. On the other hand, white holes which emit materials and are an alternative answer for an

equation satisfied by the black holes are theoretically expected to exist. The white holes have not

been confirmed yet. Both of the holes are considered to be connected with a worm hole of an ex­

tremely thin diameter, about 10· cm. Although the nuclear collapse is one of the most important
                                33




reactions in astrophysics, those experimental researches have not been progressed so far. The

reason is obvious. Ten times huge mass over that of the sun is needed to induce the nuclear col­

lapse, because the gravitational force is the weakest among four forces.

         However, the nuclear collapse in laboratory was recently discovered by the author in

1991 during experiments of so-called "cold.fusion"(} - 4). Here the nuclear collapse was directly

induced by the electromagnetic force which is 40 orders stronger than the gravitational force. So

it was callded "Electro-Nuclear Collapse(ENC)."

I.b. Electro-Nuclear Reactions(ENRs)
         During cold fusion experiments, several kinds of electro-nuclear reactions(ENRs) were

discovered by the author(l). They took place in a special state of strongly compressed hydrogen




                                              - 1 -

                                              -50-
clusters, called "itonic state," in which many electrons were degenerated and interconnected each

other so that bonding between atoms became extremely strong. The itonic cluster was a strongly

coupled multibody system so that ENRs were new nuclear reactions which were never observed

so far. There were five kinds of ENRs that were found until now: a new fusion reaction of D +

D --• •He(with no gamma rays), nuclear transmutation(EN1), generation of tiny neutron stars,

electro-nuclear multiple fission reactions(EMF) and nuclear collapse(ENC). The detail was ex­

plained in the first review paper( l ). The probability of the reactions depended on the compression

strength. The new fusion reaction generating 'He was the major reaction which predominately

contributed to the excess heat production in electrolysis cold fusion experiments with a palladium

metal and heavy water. On the other hand, ENC was the most remarkable reaction among them

and could generate tiny black and white holes. It was difficult to experimentally prove the forma­

tion of the black hole but very easy to show that of the white holes, because materials with ano­

malous structures were explained as being emitted from the white holes.

I.e. This Paper
         This review paper is one of the review series which followed a previous paper reviewing

cold fusion: "Mechanisms of Cold Fusion: Comprehensive Explanation by The Nattoh Model"

(I). This paper comprehensively explains the mechanisms of ENC based on The Extended Nattoh

Model. First an effective experimental method inducing ENC will be introduced: undenvater

spark discharges(USDs). Many experimental products will be obtained to prove ENC, each of
which will be explained by The Nattoh Model. Furthermore natural phenomena related to ENC

will be described: ball lightning and earthquakes. Finally some topics will be discussed.


2. Experiments of Underwater Spark Discharge(USD)

         It was found by the author in 1994 that remarkable tiny sparks appeared on the surface

of thin nickel wire electrodes during continuous underwater DC discharges(5 - 7). Examining in

detail, the sparks were not normal but "itonic clusters" in which ENC took place. The underwater

spark discharges(USDs) were fully developed by the author as the simplest and most effective



                                               - 2 -

                                              -51-
method for inducing ENC. Here the current density could be increased sufficiently high and
pinch effects well worked on the electrode surface. Appropriately selected electrolytes improved
the "itonization" processes which were essential to induce ENC. Furthermore the USD experi­
ments under a pulsed AC mode were conducted as experiments of ENC with a large scale.
2. a. Continuous Mode

         The continuous mode of USD was useful for examining ENC processes in detail. Many
experiments of USD were carried out with thin metal wire electrodes under the continuous DC
mode(7, 8).
         The experimental arrangement is shown in Fig. l(a). Electrodes of thin metal wires(0.3 -
 2.0 mmcp) were vertically inserted in an electrolyte solution contained in a 50 ml beaker. Pure
metals such as palladium, nickel, cadmium, iron, molybdenum, titanium, tungsten and platinum,
and some alloys were examined. For a reference electrode, a thin platinum wire(0.5 mmcp) was
usually used. Ordinary water mixed with potassium carbonate(about 1.5 Mol/1) was used for the
electrolyte solution. Heavy water was not neccesarily required(9). Discharges under the continu­
ous DC mode were employed between the electrodes. It was convenient to use the electrodes
with different effective lengths. Then the current density on a shorter electrode was higher such
that pinch effects could well work.
         A copper plate(typically about 25 x 25 mm) was used for a monitoring plate to catch up
products emitted during the discharges. Alternatively, similar copper plates were sometimes used
for a reference electrode instead of the platinum wire in order to effectively collect the products.
The products and those traces caught on the copper plates were examained with an optical micro­
scope(OM) and a scanning electron microscope(SEM). Element analysis for the products was
carried out with an energy dispersive X-ray spectroscopy(EDX), but with which no lighter ele­
ments than B were analyzed. Two dimensional mapping of elements was sometimes performed
with an electron probe microscope analyzer(EPMA). The phenomena of the discharges were also
observed by a microtelescope with the multiplication of 10 to 200 times. X-rays or gamma rays
were often monitored with a CdTe detector.



                                               - 3 -


                                               -52-
          The characteristic curve of IN was shown in Fig. 1 in Ref. 7. Here a thin palladium

metal wire(0.5 mmcp and effectively 3 mm long) and a platinum wire(0.5 mmcp and effectively

about 25 mm long) were used for the cathode and anode, respectively. The general aspects of the

figure were similar for the other metals. The amplitude of the current depended on the experi­

mental arrangement such as the effective length of the electrodes which were inserted in the solu­

tion. There were three regions in the I/V curve: ( 1) a linear region of low voltages below about

40 V, (2) a fluctuating region of mediate voltages around 40 - 50 V and (3) a pinched region of

high voltages over 50 V. In the first region, the gas evolution from the electrodes proportionally

increased as the voltage increased. In the high voltage region, the pinch effects worked on the

surface of the palladium cathode to strongly suppress the current. Simultaneously the gas evolut­

ion was also suppressed. In the mediate region, the pinch effects were sometimes broken and the

current violently fluctuated.

2.b. Pulse Mode

          The USD method with a pulsed AC mode was the most effective to induce a large scale

of ENC(5, 9 - 12). Here tiny sparks which were observed during the continuous USD could per­

fectly cover the electrode surface so that the whole electrode inserted in the solution could lead

to ENC.

          The experimental arrangement with the pulsed AC mode is shown in Fig. l(b), which

was similar to that used for the continuous DC mode. Here thin wire electrodes of cadmium(0.5

mmcp) and lead(l.0 mmcp) were used for the cathode. Ordinary water solutions of potassium car­

bonate(about 1.5 Mol/1), potassium hydroxide(about 1.5 Mol/1) and cesium carbonate(about 0.6

Mol/1) were contained in a glass petridish(inner diameter, about 60 mmcp). When the electrolyte

of potassium hydroxide was used, the system was perfectly carbon free. This was useful to exa­

mine the generation of C atoms, as will be described in Secs. 3.e. and 3.f. The effects of these

electrolytes were examined by comparing K with Ce. A box of copper plates(typically 25 mm x

25 mm x 30 mm) was used for the reference electrode. The box electrode was effective not only

for increasing the current density to induce ENC but also for catching reaction products in the



                                               - 4 -

                                               -53-
total direction. All of the copper plates was polished with diamond pastes like a mirror and

washed in alchol with ultrasonic vibration. The products were analyzed in the same manner as

during the continuous USD experiment(Sec. 2.a.).

         Discharges were employed with an electrical switch between the wire electrode and the

copper plates under the pulsed AC mode: up to 120 V, 50 Hz, 20 - 800 msec ON, 20 - 60 sec

OFF and a fixed phase of 0 deg. Here the amplitude of the potential on the thin electrode chan­

ged from Oto 120 V but forwards the negative voltage. A couple of shots were repeated. Among

the shots, the first one seemed especially effective for reactions to occur, because flashes were

the most brilliant. Although the microtelescope was connected with a monitoring TV, the speed

of recording(time step, 30 msec) was not enough for continuously following reactions during

each shot. The contents of the reactions caused by the shot had to be inferred by examining pro­

ducts deposited on the copper plates.



3. Experimental Evidence for ENC

         Many experimental evidence for ENC were obtained during a series of the USD experi­

ments with the continuous DC and the pulsed AC modes.

3. a.. Microsparks on Electrodes

         First of all we have to start with the remarkable tiny sparks which were observed on the

surface of the cathode(6 - 8). During the continuous DC discharges, extraordinary sparks began to

appear on the tip of the thin nickel wire cathode around 40 - 50 V in the mediate region of I/V

In the pinched region, the number of the sparks increased as the voltage increased, and eventually

the whole surface of the cathode was covered by the sparks. Then other sparks appeared on the

platinum wire anode in the high voltage over 60 V. By observing the details with the microtele­

scope, it was found that the tiny spark on the cathode consisted of many microsparks, each of

them was a brightly shining ball and slowly moving around on the surface. On the other hand,

the sparks on the anode seemed to be no real bodies but just lightning. The light on the anode

was weaker than that on the cathode. This fact suggested that different mechanisms should work



                                               - 5 -

                                               -54-
on the individual electrodes. A titanium electrode was exceptual among the metals used in the
experiments. With titanium, sparks first began to appear on the anode and was followed on the
cathode in a higher voltage. A special mechanisms could be suggested for titanium(Sec. 4.a).
         The misrosparks on the cathode could exist in the solution as well as in the atmosphere
as stable bodies, at least for a moment. An important picture which was shown in Fig. 4 in Ref. 8
was accidentally taken to indicate such free microsparks. While the microsparks were shining on
the nickel cathode, a tiny explosion took place and made a group of the microsparks leave the
electrode. Then they were driven toward the anode in the solution by the potential and broken
up there. This fact clearly indicated that the microsparks were negatively charged. When the 50
ml beaker was put in a bath with ultrasonic vibration, more microsparks were effectively generat­
ed and easy to leave the electrode.
3.b. Electro-Nuclear Incineration(ENI)

         Experiments of Electro-Nuclear lncineration(ENI) were attempted with a rolled ballic
anode(about 5 mmcp) of a very thin nickel metal wire(0.l mmcp x about 20 cm long) under the
continuous DC mode(7). Here the platinum wire cathode and the potassium carbonate solution
were used. When the nickel wire ball was pinched around 50 V, reaction products erupted from
the wire surface. The shape of the eruption seemed curiously conic, but hollow inside it. Most of
the conic products could not escape from the outer surface of the pinched electrode. Many pro­
ducts also arose upwards along the pinched electrode over the surface level of the solution, and
formed like white solidified materials.
         Increasing the voltage over around 70 V, the nickel metal wire began to bum and those
color became red. While the current was supplied, ENI continued underwater and eventually the
nickel wire ball completely vanished.
3.c. /tonic Clusters

         The Nattoh Model proposed that it is essential to induce ENC that hydrogen clusters
transform to a special state, "itonic state," in which many electrons are degenerated and inter­
connected each other(l). The itonic clsuters were successfully caught on nuclear emulsions in



                                              - 6 -


                                              -55-
earlier experiments of cold fuison(l3). The itonic electrons had a mesh like structure as shown
in Fig. 4 in Ref. 13 and were broken by an explosive reaction of inner hydrogen clusters as
shown in Fig. 3 in Ref. 13. Since the current density was high in the USD experiments, a lot of
the itonic clusters which involved much more electrons were observed under the continuous DC
mode as well as the pulsed AC mode(5 - 10).
         First of all, a lot of traces for the itonic electrons were observed on the copper plates
under both of the discharge modes. They were shown in Fig. 4 in Ref. 14. The traces which had
shapes like fem leaves indicated the break up of many interconnected electrons. Especially during
the continuous DC discharges, long linked electrons could be observed in a visual sight with the
microtelescope, when they left the electrode. Here the ultrasonic vibration accelerated those form­
ation(l5).
         Secondly a lot of itonic clusters involving heavy atoms were deposited on the copper
plates during USDs with both of the modes: itonic electrolytes such as K and Ce, and itonic elec­
trodes such as Pb. The electrolyte driven by the potential in the solution was itonized on the elec­
trode surface, containing hydrogen atoms. Here the structure of the itonic clusters sensitively de­
pended on its valence electrons because they could determine the number of water molecules
assembling around the electrolyte in the solution. The itonic K cluster was shown in Fig. 2 in

Ref. 14. Around the K atoms, there were scattered straight debrises of the itonic electrons, in
which the hydrogen atoms should be involved as well. The pattern of the scattered debrises sug­
gested the structure of the itonic cover: it was not uniform but had a mesh like structure. This
was consistent with the broken itonic meshes observed in the previous cold fusion experiments
(4, 13), and would be closely related to similar products obtained during the Matsumae earth­
quakes as will be described in Sec. 5.e. Furthermore there were a lot of Pb clusters deposited on
the copper plates which had thick layers of itonic Cs clusters. This will be described in Sec. 3.d.
         An extraordinary energy shift was observed for the characteristic X-rays from the itonic
clusters during the analysis with EDX(l 6). There was a partially break up of an itonic Pb cluster,
 in which itonic K atoms were somewhat involved. The EDX spectrum showed a slight energy



                                               - 7 -

                                               -56-
shift for the K-X rays of the K atoms compared with the normal one. The energy shift directly

suggested that outer orbital electrons which were itonized compressed the K atoms and made

inner electrons change their energy states.
3.d /tonic Pb Balls

         A lot of tiny itonic Pb balls were generated during USD with the pulsed AC mode.

Their photographs were shown elsewhere(l 7). Here a thin lead wire (1 mmcp) and ordinary water

mixed with potassium carbonate and hydroxide, and cesium carbonate were used for an electrode

and an electrolyte solution, respectively. While most of the Pb balls were deposited on the

bottom copper plate of a box electrode, some balls were caught even on the lateral or top plates.

Many of them were itonized so that some kinds of ENRs could be expected to occur in the balls.

Some of the Pb balls remained with slight distortions and others were partially or perfectly

broken up. It should depend on the rate of the itonization. The balls with ENRs could be easily

distinguished from Pb debrises which were just molten.

         In a low itonization rate, the compression should not have been enough to induce ENC.

Then the Pb balls were slightly distorted but the itonization processes certainly proceeded even

in the ball, as shown in Fig. 1 in Ref 17. In the SEM view, the itonized Pb clusters seemed like

being coated with thin and smooth Pb films, which were caused by the itonized electrons.

         In a high itonization rate, ENC was drastically induced during which the reaction was

explosive to expand the Pb cluster from the inside. Then hollow Pb balls were resulted by the

explosion, as shown in Fig. 18 in Ref. 17. Here a lot of tiny Pb grains appeared on a spherical

surface of the balls. The grains oppositely suggested that the lead metal was really compressed.

It was extraordinary that the tiny grains did not scatter out and that the hollow balls did not fall.

The spherical shape of the balls was sustained by their inner thin films, which were one product

of ENC and consisted of mainly C atoms. The detail of the films will be described in Sec. 3.f.

         A lot of Pb balls which were combined each other were also observed. There were three

kinds of those balls. The first was simply made by a collision between two itonic Pb balls while

they were flying during the discharges. The second was made during the ENC process, as shown



                                                  8


                                               -57-
in Figs. 16 and 17 in Ref 17. Here the itonic Pb cluster pushed out the hollow Pb balls which

was mentioned above, just like a making process of popcorns. The third was assemblies of plural

 itonic Pb clusters, as shown in Figs. 21 and 22 in Ref 17. They were probably made either dur­

ing the itonizatin process of several clusters or during the recombination process between Pb

clusters and itonic Ce atoms which were scattered during the discharges. Anyway thick itonic

layers of Ce atoms were obviously seen over the Pb balls in the figures just like fermented soy­

beans ("Nattoh" in Japanese) as was predicted by The Nattoh Model(l , 18).

3.e. Generation of Carbon Tubes

         There were two kinds of remarkable products which were generated in the itonic clusters

: C tubes and films( 6). The C films will be described in Sec. 3 .f. First the tube like products

were shown in Figs. 25 and 26 in Ref. 17. They were caught on the copper plates during the

USO experiments with both of the modes. The tube products seemed to be more generated during

the continuous DC mode than the pulsed AC mode. There could be two kinds of tube products:

thin rolling tubes(typically 5 µm) as shown in Figs. 25 and 26 in Ref 17 and Fig. 6 in Ref 14,

and thin flat tubes(typically 50 µm wide).

         The EDX analysis revailed that all the tubes consisted of mainly an element of C. The

typical EDX spectrum was shown in Fig. 9(a) in Ref 12. An element of O also appeared in the

spectrum but could be somewhat contaminated from molecules of water. In several cases, other

heavy elements such as Ti and Fe were also observed along the tubes. The EDX spectra at dif­

ferent locations of a tube were shown in Fig. 9(b) in Ref 12, in which heavy elements such as

Fe, Ca and S were observed near the center of the tip(Fig. 8(b)) while light elements such as C

and O were observed on the peripheral surface of the tip(Fig. 8(a)). This fact suggested that some

centrifugal effects could have worked during generating the tubes. An important figure for the

tube was shown in Fig. 6 in Ref 14. The tube was cut into several pieces for the presentation.

An enlarged view of the top row was shown in Fig. 6(b) in Ref 14, in which an electrode cluster

was associated with the tube. It obviously indicated that the tube was generated in the cluster and

emerged from there. It should be difficult to consider the mechanisms for the generation of such



                                                  9

                                               -58-
curious tubes in the cluster. A clear explanation will be described in Sec. 4.f., based on The

Nattoh Model.
3.f. Generation of Carbon Films
         The second remarkable product in the itonic cluster was C films, as shown in Figs. 23

and 24 in Ref. 17. They were generated during the USD experiments with both of the modes, but

seemed more frequently produced with the pulsed AC mode. The films were very thin and had

various shapes due to folding effects. The EDX analysis also indicated that all the films consisted

of mainly C and 0, similarly to the tubes. The typical spectrum for the film was shown in Ref.

15. Various elements such as 0, Mg, Al,' Si, S, Cl. Ti and Fe were observed in some cases. Then

 the relative distribution of the elements changed for the individual film. When the solution of

potassium hydroxide was used, the experimental system became perfectly carbon free. Even then,

 the C films were found in the same manner. It was obvious that the C element was newly gen­

erated by some nuclear reaction during the discharges. Some important pictures which suggested

the generation mechanisms of the C films were obtained. They were shown in Fig.s. 14 and 15

in Ref. 17. Here the films were associated with an itonic Pb cluster or a hollow Pb ball. In some

cases, the hollow Pb ball directly emerged from the itonic Pb cluster, as shown in Figs. 16 and

17 in Ref. 17.

         Similar thin films were already observed on nuclear emulsions in previous experiments

of cold fusion{4). Figure 4 in Ref. 4 clearly showed the generation of thin films with ring or

spherical shapes during an inner epxplosive reaction. Compared with the thickness of the nuclear

emulsion, about 100 µm, the film was very thin. Then the film was asigned to be generated by

the nuclear collapse of guard neutrons.

3.g. Generation of Ring Products

         Curious tiny rings were produced during the USD experiments with a thin Fe cathode(8).

 A small scale of explosion took place during the continuous DC discharges. A lot of rings were

caught on the surface of the iron electrode. They were shown in Fig. 8 in Ref. 8. The diamerer of

 the rings was typically about 50 µmcp. The ring could sustain their shapes for a couple of days



                                              - 10 -

                                              -59-
and eventually decayed to a regular hexagonal plate, as shown in Fig. 9 in Ref. 8. Then the inner
 region of the ring shrinked to the center at which corona like discharges were emitted.
           The EPMA analysis showed that a Cd element circularly distributed in the ring. Since
the system was perfectly Cd free, the Cd element was generated by some nuclear process. The
ring products were not obtained so far in other systems except with the iton wire.
3.h. Electro-Nuclear Transmutation(EN1)
           The phenomena of Electro-Nuclear Transmutation(EN1) were observed not only dur­
ing the cold fusion experiments(l9) but also during the USD experiments(12, 15). During USD,
the pulsed AC discharges were especially useful for examining ENT, because itonic clusters in
which ENT took place were separately observed from clusters involving ENC. The clusters with
ENT were caught on the copper plates which were located in the solution as well as over the sol­
ution during the USD experiment with the lead wire electrode. Photographs were presented in Fig.
 27 in Ref. 17 and Fig. 10 in Ref. 12. Elements such as Al, Si and Fe observed in the hydrogen
clusters. Among the elements, the electrolyte of K should have been much more, if they were just
 impurities contained in the solution. They were transmuted in the itonic hydrogen cluster. Fur­
thermore a strange phenomenon was observed associated with the ENT cluster: there was an inert
 zone around the transmuted materials only on the copper plates which were inserted in the solu­
tion. This fact suggested that the itonic clusters should have had the capability of making material
 surfaces somewhat inert. This effect should be caused by the itonization process on the material
surface.
3.i Electro-Nuclear Electro-Magnetic Effects(ENEM)
           During the USD experiments, the electro-magnetic conditions of the system could be
effected by induced ENRs. They could be c�led "Electro-Nuclear Electro-Magnetic Effects
(ENEM)." Three kinds of ENEM were observed so far: the electric power generation, a new kind
 of electrical current and the magnetization of electrode.
           First the electric power generation could be made by USD. When the itonic cluster in­
duced ENRs in itself, nuclear energy could be transferred to the vibrational energy of the itonized



                                               - 11 -

                                               -60-
 electrons, without emitting energetic radiations such as gamma rays or neutrons. When the iton­
ic cluster broke up, furthermore, orbital electrons could be released as free electrons. The electric
power could be amplified in the sense of the number of electrons as well as their energy. Pheno­

mena of the electric power generation were observed during continuous AC discharges with titan­
ium bolt electrodes(l0 mmcp and effectively about 80 mm long) in the potassium carbonate solut­
ion(20, 21).
         Secondly a new kind of electical cu"ent was the transport of the itonic clusters through
 a cable wire. The itonic cluster could somewhat exsist as a stable body. Since it had the nega­
tive charges, it could be driven through a copper wire by the electrical potential. When many
clusters flew along the wire but with the conventional electrical current, they could seem like a
new kind of the electrical current. Here the current should be perfectly different from the conven­
tional one, because the fairly massive itonic clusters could really flow through the cable wire.
         In the previous USD experiments(22), phenomena of the new kind of the current were
observed. Thin copper cable wires which were coated with thin polyvinyl films were used to
connect an electrode with a power supply. The copper wires near the connecting points of the
electrode became dark. As the voltage or the discharge period increased, the color became darker
and the colored area wider. The length of the colored area was about 30 cm at maximum. Here
elements of K and Ca were confirmed by EDX. Since K was the eletrolyte element, this fact ob­
viously indicated that the itonic hydrogen clusters containg K ran along the copper cable wires,
but inside the coating films. The element of Ca should be transmuted from K in the itonic clus­
ters during the transportation(}).
         The third was the magnetization of electrodes. The itonic cluster itself should be so
strongly magnetized by its closed electrons like a microscopic magnet. During the USD experi­
ments, the itonic clusters could be deposited on the electrode surface and some of them could
enter the bulk. As a result, the electrode could be magnetized. Such phenomena were observed
with a thin nickel wire electrode(22). The material showed the magnetization.
3.j. Radiations



                                               - 12 -

                                               -61-
          Two kinds of radiations were observed during the USD experiments (8): energetic X­

rays and unknown radiations. Energetic X-rays were observed by the CdTe detector under the the

 continuous DC mode. The X-rays were associated with the appearance of the tiny sparks on the

electrode surface and the intensity increased as the number of the sparks increased. The energy

spectrum of the X-rays was continuous and decreased monotonically as the energy increased up

to about 130 keV. The X-rays spectra were shown in Fig. 6 in Ref 8. Such energetic X-rays

could not be emitted from conventional orbital electrons at all. However it could be emitted by

the break up of the itonic electron linkage, those traces were very often observed on the copper

plates( 14).

          On the other hand, unknown radiations were observed during the continuous USD.

They were emitted from near the cadmium cathode on those surface the tiny sparks were shining.

 Here a special device, the Egely's wheel, was used to detect the unknown radiation(23). The

wheel could respond to unknown biological radiations from the hands of a human being. When

he or she were vital, the wheel indicated a high value for the radiation. When the wheel was lo­

cated close to the cathode, it indicated a very high value. The radiation from USD should be

same as that of being emitted from the hands of a human being. The detailed asignment of the

unknown radiation was not made yet.

          Visible light, which was emitted from the tiny sparks. might have some information so

that analyses with an optical spectroscopy(OS) were attempted(24). The spectra which were ob­

tained from the thin nickel cathode had no isolated lines but a very smooth hill shape.

3.k Phenomena Like Ball Lightning(BL)

          During the pulsed USD experiments, two kinds of Ball Lightning(BL) like phenomena

were observed: itonic clusters with curious voltex traces and ring products.

          First many traces for BL were recorded on the copper plate electrode during USD of the
 pulsed AC mode(14). They were itonic clusters which were generated during USD and jumped

over the surface level of the solution. The itonic clusters left traces of a nearly circular disk, but

sometimes with a slight distortion. Associated with the itonic clusters, there were curious voltex



                                                - 13 -


                                                -62-
traces. This was inferred that thin C tubes which were emitted from a white hole ran around in
the cluster.
           Secondly, ring products which also ran around like BL on the surface of nuclear emul­
sions were early observed(9). The generation of the ring prodcuts was described in Sec. 3.g.


4. Explanation by The Nattoh Model

           The Nattoh Model was initially proposed by the author in order to comprehensively ex­
plain the mechanisms of cold fusion, mainly for the method of electrolysis method with a palla­
dium cathode(!). The model proposed a new state of hydrogen cluster, "itonic cluster," and seve­
ral kinds of ENRs occurring in the cluster. Since ENC was the most remarkable reaction among
ENRs, the USD method was developed to investigate ENC in detail. As resulted, the remarkable
nuclear regeneration(ENG) was newly discovered so that the model was required to extensively

involve related physics. Here the comprehensive explanation for ENC based on The Extended
Nattoh Model will be described mainly for the USD experiments mentioned in the preceding
chapter.
4. a. ltonization Processes

           According to The Nattoh Model, it is essential to induce ENRs that a hydrogen cluster
should be strongly compressed to transform to an itonic state. Here many electrons are degenerat­
ed and interconnected each other to strongly bind hydrogen atoms. The compression is so strong
that it makes various kinds of ENRs occur in the itonic cluster. The itonic clusters were really
confirmed during the USD experiments(S - 10). The microsparks which were separated from the
electrode left traces with various shapes on the copper plates. The traces were important evidence
 for the formation of the itonic cluster. There the microsparks on the electrode themselves were
such itonic clusters. There were some kinds of itonic clusters: itonic electrons, itonic hydrogen
clusters, itonic electrolyte clusters, and itonic electrode clusters, including those mixtures. They
all were obseved during the USD experiments mentioned in the preceding chapters.
           There seem to be two processes for the itonization: on the electrode and in the electrode.



                                               - 14 -

                                               -63-
 The first is the itonization on the electrode surface. It takes place between electrons, hydrogens
and electrolytes which are gathered on the electrode surface by the potential. The second is the
itonization in the electrode during which the electrode material itself becomes itonized. The two
processes should be distinguished.
         First the itoniza,tion process on the electrode surface is associated with the pinch effect.
 It is well observed with the 100 % reproducibility during the USD experiments with the conti­
nuous DC mode. In the high voltage over about 50 V, the pinch effect works well on the cathode
 surface. Here the hydrogen atoms are so strongly compressed by the electrical potential that the
evolution of the hydrogen gas is also completely suppressed. The hydrogen atoms should be in a
critical state of gas-liquid mixtures. Furthermore the electrical current are enforced to flow over
the hydrogen clusters. This is a key point for the itonization of the clusters. Many surplus elec­
trons could be degenerated and interconnected each other to form the itonic electrons and clusters.
 On the electrode surface, the electrolyte such as K can be driven by the potential to become
itonic clusters, surrounded by the hydrogen atoms. This direct compression by the electrical pot­
ential could be much stronger than that with the eletrolyzing method with a palladium rod, in
which hydrogen atoms were indirectly compressed in grain boundaries in the metal.
         During the itonization process, an eddy current might have taken an important role.
Figure 6 in Ref. 17 showed a lead ring with voltex like patterns, in which tiny Pb grains were
generated. When the lead electrode was cut by the pulsed AC discharges, surplus electrons could
form eddy current on the electrode surface to be itonized.
         Secondly the itoniza,tion process in the electrode bulk could take place in a high current
 density, i.e., during the pulsed AC discharges. In general, when the current is flown in a metal,
electrons run on the surface of the metal rather than in the bulk. Since the metal usually has a
polycrystalline structure, the current flows not only on the metal surface but also through grain
boundaries in the metal. When many electrons are overflown in the electrode such as lead, the
itonization process could take place through the grain boundaries in the metal. Those itonization
phenomena were often observed during the pulsed USD experiments with the thin lead wire elec-



                                               - 15 -

                                               -64-
trode(l 7). In broken Pb balls, strange Pb layers like thin films were formed, as shown in Figs. I
to 3 in Ref. 17. Here they showed the itonization process which proceeded through the grain
boundaries of the lead metal. Alternatively tiny Pb clusters like stars were also observed for
completely broken Pb balls, as shown in Figs. 9 and 10 in Ref 17. All of them were clear ex­
perimental evidence for the second itonization process.
         During both of the itonization processes, the itonization should take place mainly for
outer orbital electrons of the atoms. As resulted by the itonic compression, inner orbital electrons
should also be affected to have somewhat different energy levels. The extraordinary energy shift
of X-rays from the inner orbital electrons should be caused by the itonic compression, as men­
tioned in Sec. 3.c. In a high current density, more outer orbital electrons should be involved in
the itonization process so that the energy shift for the inner orbital electrons increase. It is sug­
gested that the information of the extraordinary energy shift for K-X rays could be useful to
examine the details of the electrical structure of the itonic clusters.
         The titanium electrode was special among the metals used in the experiments. Sparks
first appeared not on the cathode but on the anode. Here the spark was discharges caused by the
removal of brittle materials on the electrode surface. At the sparking point, a tiny white area of
the titanium metal was seen with an optical microscope.
4. b. ENRs in /tonic Clusters
         Since the itonic cluster is a system of strongly coupled multibodies, several new kinds of
 nuclear reactions are possible in the cluster. ENRs were explained in detail in the preceding re­
view paper(l). The new reactions are very different from the conventional nuclear reactions
which occur in an essentially isolated system. Furthermore the kind of ENRs depends on the
bonding strength of the itonic cluster which varies with the number of involved electrons. There
are four kinds of ENRs which were found so far: (1) a new nuclear fusion reaction between two
deuteriums, (2) the nuclear transmutation(ENT), (3) the generation of multiple neutron nuclei and
 (4) the nuclear collapse forming tiny black and white holes(ENC).
         The first is the new nuclear fusion reaction which produces • He from two D nuclei: D



                                                - 16 -

                                                -65-
 + D --• • He(no gamma rays). The reaction seems to violate the conservation law of momentum
 so that it cannot be allowed in isolated systems at all. However it is possible in the itonic clus­
ter because the momentum can be received by the whole body of the itonic cluster just like the
Mossbauer effect. Since many other hydrogens involved in the reaction work as catalysts, the
reaction was called "Hydrogen-Catalyzed Fusion Reaction" in the previous review paper. Energy
 released from the fusion reaction is transferred to the vibration of the itonic electrons so that no
radiations are emitted. The excess heat which was obtained from the electrolysis of heavy water
with a palladium metal was predominately caused by this reaction(25). So the method could be
calld "L'old Fusion."
            The second of ENRs is the electro-nuclear transmutation(ENT) and will be described in
Sec. 4.c.
            The third of ENRs is the generation of tiny multiple neutron nuclei. Here the nucleus
consists of only neutrons like a neutron star. The neutron nucleus is generated by electron cap­
tures during the compression of the itonization process. Although the neutron nucleus should be
unstable, it is covered by the itonic mesh so that it can exist for a moment as a stable body.
When the itonic mesh is broken, multiple neutrons could be instantaneously scattered in all direc­
tion. The break up of those neutron nuclei was observed on nuclear emulsions by the auhtor(3).
The multiple neutrons were really measured with a coincidence method of neutron detection by a

 group of Srinivasan, in which the multiple neutron nucleus was called by a different name of
"bunched neutrons"(26). Although the third of ENRs might be important in some fields such as
coherent neutron generation, the study was not progressed so far because most researchers con­
tinued to look for the 2.5 MeV neutrons generated from the conventional D + D fusion reaction.
            The fourth, the most remarkable of ENRs, is the nuclear collapse(ENC) and will be
mentioned in Sec. 4.d.
4.c. Electro-Nuclear Transmutation(EN1)
            The second of ENRs is the electro-nuclear transmutation(EN1) in which one or more
elements are converted to other ones. In 1991, the author first found that the nuclear transmuta-



                                               - 17 -


                                               -66-
tion took place in grain boundaries in a palladium metal rod which was used for a cold fusion
experiment(l 9). Then the other co-author of the paper who helped me to analyze elements in a
released gas did not agree with the author's idea of ENT. Later, a group of Kucherov also re­
ported ENT with many elements during discharges in gas(27). Recently, many researchers ob­
served ENT with various methods(28). The remarkable properties of ENT are (I) the transmuta­
tion rate is extremely high compared with other devices such as a nuclear reactor or accelerator
and (2) no energetic radiations such as gamma rays are associated as mentioned before.
         There are several processes for ENT which all occur in the itonic cluster: (1) capturing
