Ref. (74)
                    "Comments to Electro-Nuclear Reactions"

                            Takaaki MATSUMOTO
                         Department of Nuclear Engineering
                         Hokkaido University, Sapporo 060-0813, JAPAN
                  (Fusion Technology, submitted on February 10, 1999)

          I would like to comment to Electro-Nuclear Reactions, including the response to a paper
by the group of Ohmori et al. (1) and a letter from Lewis (2), both of which discussed the
author's works.
          Let us consider a situation in which lots of electrons were enforcaj to charge over an
atomic cluster which was isolated. What kind of state did the cluster become? The Nattoh Model
(3, 4) predicted the formation of a special cluster, called "itonic" cluster, in which lots of surplus
electrons did not scatter out but they were interconnected with each other and probably
degenerated. The bonding with those electrons was so strong that nuclear reactions took place in
the cluster. The reactions were called "Electro-Nuclear Reactions(ENRs)," because the nuclear
reactions were directly induced by the electric force with no process of accelerating particles.
Several kinds of ENRs were found so far(4).
          First, one example of ENRs was Electro-Nuclear Transmutation (ENT). The author first
discovered ENT for various elements in a palladium rod during the electrolysis of heavy water(5).
Then many researchers opposed to ENT by the reason of the too amazing results, saying that the
elements observed should be impurities. Even a co-author of the paper(5), Kurokawa, did not
believe ENT. However, many reports which supported ENT continued to be published until now
with various devices(see the proceedings of ICCF). The paper(l) by the group of Ohmori et al.
also obtained a result which supported the author's claim of ENT, by originally finding some
deviation of isotopic composition with a Mills type device. They confirmed that the reaction of
ENT locally occurred in craters, as the author earlier indicated(5). But they claimed that ENT took
place in craters with curious shapes like volcanoes and that the author obtained little unambiguous
evidence to support some nuclear reactions because the author's craters had not volcano like
shapes(6). They distorted the fact. The main object of the paper(6) was to verify what kind of
nuclear reactions took place and it was succeeded. Lots of pictures indicating the nuclear collapse
were taken on nuclear emulsion, shown in Fig.s. (5) to (7) of Ref. (6). The nuclear collapse was
really a nuclear reaction. Nuclear emulsion which seemed unfamiliar to the electrochemists was
one of the most sensitive and popular techniques to examine nuclear reactions. They did not
consciously refer the author's paper(5) in which an element analysis similar to the group's paper(l)
was made around craters. A detailed comparison between the craters was made in the letter of
Lewis(2). Here attention should be paied to the property of the electrode materials: Pd, Ni and Au.
Since Pd had the high permeability of hydrogen, ENRs took palce deeper under the surface(3). A
big crater of a about 2 mm diameter was generated on the backside of a thin palladium foil ( 0.05
mm thick), as partially shown at a upper and left corner in Fig. 8 (a) of Ref. (7) (but no


                                                - 1 -


                                              -272-
description was given) . The crater was associated with a curious: assembly which was generated
by simultaneous ENC reactions. The shape of the craters was not a so important problem. The
group continued the discharges for 30 days for the case with the craters like volcanoes, whilist
only about l.5 hours were consumed in the author's case. During their longer period, more
reaction products were accumulated around reaction places, as described by them.
         Secondly I would like to point out a much more important fact the group missed: the
formation of the itonic cluster which was essential to induce ENT. When the itonic cluster was
generated with a high current density, it began to shine and its lightning could easily be seen; for
example, during underwater spark discharge(USD) experiments in which an thin. electrode was
pinched(8). Recently, the group again followed the author's USD experiment of having a strong
nonlinear IN curve caused by the formation of pinched electrodes(9). They had to increase the
current higher, due to a larger electrode. There they will soon recognize similar clusters.
Furthermore the missing made them insufficiently understand the mechanisms of ENT. They
proposed Eqs. (2) to (14) in Ref. 1 for ENT, which was based on an idea of that a compound
nucleus caused by absorbing many protons could induce multibody fission reactions. The idea was
already proposed by The Nattoh Model, although no specified equations were written. Not only
the idea but also experimental evidence were provided by the author, as shown in one of the
author's earlier experiments(lO) and in Fig. 8 of Ref. (6). The equations of (2) to (14) in Ref. 1
proposed by the group could not escape from the feasibility of emitting high energy gamma rays.
Intensive neutrons also should be emitted from Eq. (15) in Ref. 1. But experimental facts related
to the radiations which were obtained by many researchers so far were not consistent with the
prediction. This inconsistence could be easily resolved by accepting the formation of the itonic
clusters which could carry away nuclear energy. Their understanding of The Nattoh Model was
not enough yet. It is· more important to see the essential principle rather than the surface
phenomena.
         Tirdly another good example of ENRs was so-called cold fusion phenomena(CF), in
which the extraordinary generation of excess heat was published by Fleischmann and Pons in
1989. CF became very controvertial and confusive. Ten years passed since the publication. The
device was improved with a high reproducibility so that the fact of the excess heat generation was
confirmed by many researchers(see the proceedings of ICCF). But several years among them were
wasted for searching for products of the conventional D-D fusion reaction such as 2.5 MeV
neutrons. The trials failed completely. The group of Ohmori et el. also continued to claim a wrong
idea(the crack model) for a long time. It is now obvious that the generation of the excess heat was
mainly caused by a new fusion reaction as The Nattoh Model earlier proposed: D + D produced
He-4. The fusion reaction took place in the itonic cluster which was a strongly coupled multibody
system. No high energy gamma rays were emitted, because the momentum could be received by
the whole body of the itonic cluster like the Mossbauer effect. The author's claim has been
ignored for ten years, but the number of researchers who accepted the fundamental ideas of The
Nattoh Model is recently increasing. The group of Ohmori et al. was one of the researchers who
changed their ideas. But, as mentioned above, the group unfairly invaded the author's scientific


                                               -2-


                                             -273-
priorities for three points: the discovery of ENT, the idea of the multibody fission reaction caused
by absorbing many protons and the effective ENR device with the pinched electrodes.
         Fourthly, I would like to comment to the most significant reaction of ENRs,
Electro-Nuclear Collapse(ENCX4). ENC was furthermore followed by the phenomenon, called
Electro-Nuclear Regeneration(ENG). The experimental details will be soon published in Fusion
Technology. In this letter, a clear evidence for ENG is introduced as shown in Fig. I, which was
obtained during pulse AC discharges(} I). Figure I obviously indicates that a cluster of cadmium
was strongly compressed and that a thin tube emerged from the inside of the cluster, as a result of
ENC of cadmium. The element analysis with EDX showed that the film consisted of mainly
carbon. It was extremely amazing that the conventional elements such as carbon and iron were
regenerated from ENC. It was reasonable to consider that ENG which could contribute to ENT in
a broad sense also took place in the craters observed by the group of Ohmori et al. When we
specify the schemes of ENT, ENG should be precisely accounted. But it would be premature at
the present time because unexpected elements suddenly appeared during ENG.
         Fifthly, the itonic cluster could exist for a moment as a stable body and run around
underwater as well as in air. Moreover, it w� kicked back through leading wires of copper, since
it had negative charges. Those curious behavior very well resembled Ball Lightning(BL) which
was sometimes observed in the natural environment( 12). Lewis has been discussing the similarity
between BL, CF and other natural phenomena by the term of "plasmoids." Plasmoids were locally
generated in a plasma by the macroscopic mechanisms of electro-hydrodynamics, whilist the
itonic cluster could be generated from normal condensed materials by the microscopic
mechanisms. Since it was very easy to generate lots of the itonic clusters and to control them, the
study for the properties of the natural BL could be significantly progressed from now.
Alternatively, by scaling up with a high power supply, the artificial BL with a large diameter, at
least like a tennis ball, could be easily generated in laboratory. The application might be widely
opened such as to an effective device of power generation. I hoped that Fusion Technology could
actively accept many papers related to BL(l3).
         Lastly, Roentgen discovered X-rays in the end of the 19th century. It stimulated the
microscopic study for the atomic world of IO -s cm in the 20th century and was followed by the
Rutherford's discovery of the nuclear world of IO • 13 cm. Now the quark world of IO • 11 cm is
facing a high barrier. On the other hand, the discovery of ENC opened the door to a super
microscopic world of IO •33 cm at a stretch. Subnuclear physics will be widen to the field between
                 33
IO • cm and I0" cm in the 21th century.

Ref. (78) Proc. of Int. Symp. of Ball Lightning (ISBL'99),
          Antwerp, p. 249 (1999).
        Micro Ball Lightning Generated During Underwater Spark Discharges

                                   Takaaki MATSUMOTO
                               Department of Nuclear Engineering
                                      Hokkaido University
                                   Sapporo 060-0813, JAPAN
Abstract
        Experiments of Underwater Spark Dicharge(USD) were carried out under a pulse AC
mode with electrodes of the metal wires of cadmium, iron and gold. A counter electrode of a
copper box was used for catching reaction products during USO. Ordinary water mixed with
potassium carbonate and hydroxide were used as an electrolyte solution. Two kinds of remarkable
products casued by Electro-Nuclear Collapse were obtained: thin carbon films and tubes. The
mechanisms of ENC are also discussed by The Nattoh Model.

1. Introduction
          The Underwater Spark Discharge(USD) was one of the most effective methods for arti­
ficially generatin_g Ball Lightning(BL) in laboratory( l ). The principle was completely different
from other methods such as based on plasma states. BL which was generated by USO with a
small power device was called micro BL, but it could exist for a fairly long time as a stable body
and showed very curious behavior like natural BL.
          Two different kinds of micro BL were found so far: ballic and ring BL. The ballic BL,
a direct product of USO, was examined in detail and was found to consist of a special state of
an atomic cluster, called "itonic" cluster(2, 3). Here many electrons were assumed to be inter
connected with each other, and the bonding of those electrons was so strong that several kinds
of nuclear reactions took place in the cluster: (a) a new fusion reaction of producing He-4 from
two deuterons with no gamma rays, (b) nuclear transmutation by capturing many protons and
electrons, (c) generation of multiple neutrons(tiny neutron star) and (d) nuclear collapse and re
generation. On the other hand, the ring BLwas the second product of USO, in which no explosive
reactions occurred(2).
          This paper describes an experiment of the pulse AC-USO with electrodes of thin metal
wires. A similar experiment with a lead wire was already carried out with many remarkable re­
sults(4, 5). In this paper, thin wires of cadmium, iron and gold were used as an electrode. Results
not only duplicated the important parts of the previous ones but also provided clear evidence to
_explain the mechansims.

2. USD Experiments
         The pulse AC-USO experiment was carried out to generate micro BL in laboratory.
A similar arrangement as in the previous one(4) (with a thin lead wire electrode) was applied,
as shown in Fig. 1. Thin wire electrodes (1.0 mm cf> x about 10 mm long) of cadmium(99.9999
% pure), iron(99.998 %) and gold (99.95 % ) was used in this experiment, which was vertically
placed in an electrolyte solution. Ordinary water mixed with potassium hydroxide and cesium

                                              - 1 -

                                             -276-
carbonate(about 1.5 Mol/l) were used as electrolyte solutions. The effective length of the wire
those portion were inserted in the solution was about 2 - 3 mm so that it enhanced the current
density. A counter electrode was a cubic box(25 mm x 25 mm x 25 mm) of five or six plates of
copper(0.2 mm thick) which covered the central wire electrode. The copper plates were simulta­
neously used to catch up products emittin_g durin_g USD. All of the copper plates were polished
with diamond pastes and washed in an alcholic solution in a bath with ultrasonic vibration.
         Discharges were _performed between the wire and the box electrodes with an electrical
switch: 120 V(50 Hz), 20 msec ON, 20-30 sec OFF and phase=0 deg(fixed). A pinched region
was generated over the wire electrode by the potential. in which molecules of decomposed gas
was strongly compressed to form micro BL including the atoms of the electrode as well as the
solution. As resulted by nuclear reactions in micro BL, explosive reactions associated with lightn­
ing occurred on the tip of the electrode. The details of the mechanisms were examined in the
previous experiment with the continuous DC-USD, changing the polarity of the wire electrode(2.
3).
         Products during USD which were caught on the coper plates were analyzed by an optic­
al microscope(OM) and a scanning electron microscope(SEM). Analysis of elements was per­
formed with an energy dispersive X-ray analyzer(EDX). The window of EDX which was made
of a special material enabled us to analyze light elements until C.

3. Experimental Results
          As resulted by the pulse AC-USD experiment, many remarkable products were obtained.
  They were classified into three cate_gories: (1) micro BL and its break up, (2) thin carbon films
and (3) thin carbon tubes.
          First, lightning during the pulse AC-USD was an effective indicator of reactions. As the
generation of the itonic clusters was more effective, lightning was more bright. Lightning during
the discharges with cadmium and gold are compared in Fig. 1. Since the cadmium atoms were
reactive to hydrization, the cadmium atoms as well as the electrolyte ones could be well itonized.
On the other hand, the gold atoms could be hardly itonized so that only moderate reactions
occurred with week lightning.
          Secondly, many micro BL were observed on the copper plates. There were two kinds of
the clusters: electrode cluster and electrolyte one. The former was formed by the itonization of
the electrode by electron erosion into a bulk and was usually covered by the latter. The latter was
formed on the electrode surface by the compression of the electrolyte atoms together with de­
composed gas. A typical clsuter of the former is shown in Fig. 3, in which the cadmium elec­
trode and the cesium carbonate solution were used for USD. A spherical core of cadmium was
covered by the itonic electrolyte(cesium carbonate), which was partially broken up at a bombard­
ment on the plate. Figure 4 shows the slight break up of the cadmium electrode cluster, in which
some explosive reaction was suggested to occur. For the electrolyte cluster, on the other hand, the
bonding of the cluster was sensitive to the valence electrons of the electrolyte which determined
the number of water molecules associated with the electrolyte atom in the solution. When the
bonding was not so strong, the cluster was easily destroyed on the surface of the copper plate. A



                                               - 2 -

                                              -277-
typical case with the potassium hydroxide solution is shown in Fig. 5, in which the gold elec­
trode was used. The electrolyte cluster was scattered out into many small debris. Since gold was
less reactive with hydrogen, it was difficult to obtain clusters of the former type . For the cesium
carbonate solution with the strong bonding, on the other hand, it was easy to obtain the clusters
of the former. Figure 6 shows the break up of the electrolyte cluster of cesium carbonate,
           Thirdly, one of the remarkable results was the generation of thin films which consisted
of mainly carbon. Figure 7 shows one of the typical films. A lot of such films were observed on
the copper plate. The analysis with EDX confirmed that the films consisted of mainly carbon
element and that oxy_gen and other heavy elements such as iron were also included. Most of the
films were observed separatedly from the electrode clusters, but some were obtained associated
with the cluster, as shown in Fig. 8. The figure clearly indicates the mechanisms of the genera­
tion of the films. The film was generated in the cadmium cluster and emerged from there. The
number of the films which were associated with the cluster was not so large,
           Fourthly, the other remarkable result was the generation of thin tubes which also con­
sisted of maily carbon. The thin tube of carbon was also generated in the cadmium cluster and
emerged from there. Figure 9 shows a clear evidence for the fact. The elements of the tube were
analyzed with EDX: C and O were found. Figure 10 shows a faily long tube which was also _ge­
nerated with the cadmium electrode and the cessium carbonate solution. The left tip of the tube is
expanded in F�. 11. The fi_gure clearly indicated that the tube was folded to have a cross sec­
tion of a literature of U.


4. Discussion
           In the previous similar experiments of USD with a lead electrode, many remarkable re­
sults were obtained on copper plates(4, 5). The most remarkable one was the generation of the
thin films and tubes which consisted of maily carbon. We obtained many figures which clearly
indicated that the films and tubes were generated in the lead clusters and that thery emerged from
there. Since a system of USD was carbon free with the potassium hydroxide solution, it was con­
sidered that the C element was transmuted from the Pb element by nuclear reactions. Furthermore,
it was concluded that "Electro-Nuclear Collapse(ENC)" reactions were induced by the discharges
to contribute the element transmutation. called "Electro-Nuclear Regeneration(ENG)_" Similar
thin films were already found associated with explosions on nuclear emulsions in earlier experi­
ment(6).
           The present experiments with cadmium, iron and gold duplicated the previous results of
the generation of the thin carbon films and tubes. It was reconfirmed that the thin films and tubes
were generated in the electrode clusters and that they emerged from there. The clusters which
were associated with the thin films and tubes clearly indicated the facts. In the present experi­
ment, we found the tube of an approciate length which was associated with the cadmium cluster,
as shown in Fig. 9. The shape of the films was very various, since the films were folded or tilted
during the explosion. The shape of the tubes shown in Figs. 10 and 11 was also folded during
the generation to have the cross section of a literature of U.
           In the previous paper(2), the mechanisms were discussed for the generation of the thin



                                                - 3 -

                                               -278-
carbon films and tubes, based on The Nattoh Model. During USD, ENC took place in the center
of the cluser and generated a tiny black hole. Since the electromagnetic force is 40 orders strong­
er than the gravitational force, the nuclei could be easily destroyed. The black hole was so small
that instantaneously exploded to generate the thin spherical films of carbon. In a highly itonized
cluster, the compression by the electromagnetic force was so strong that a tiny white hole could
be formed, from which the thin carbon tube was generated. Here the nuclei should be completely
destroyed during the transport through a worm hole of I o-n cm so that the elements such as C, 0,
and Fe shoulde be regenerated from extremely elementary particles. More discussions about this
process, ENG, were described in the review paper of Ref 2.

                           Transport of Micro Ball Li_ghtning
                                   Takaaki MATSUMOTO
                               Department of Nuclear Engineering
                                     Hokkaido University
                                  Sapporo 060-0813, JAPAN
Abstract
         This paper describes experiments for the transport of micro Ball Lightning(BL) which
was generated during underwater spark dischar_ges. Here micro BL was formed on a tip of two
pinched anodes: a thin palladium wire and a thin copper plate like a knife. While micro BL was
derived towards another tip of the anode by the potential, micro BL or traees were left on the
wire or the plate. One of the remarkable products was an eruption with a speedy rotation from
moving micro BL, just like "micro tornado." Furthermore, thin carbon films and tubes were gene­
rated from nuclear collapses of micro BL during its transport. The mechanisms of the results are
explained by The Nattoh Model.

1. Introduction
          The Nattoh Model was proposed by the author to explain the mechanisms of so-called
Cold Fusion(CF) phenomena(!, 2), which predicted the formation of a special state of an atomic
cluster, called itonic cluster(3). Since Electro-Nucelar Reactions(ENRs) causing the CF phenom­
ena took place in the palladium rod of a cathode during electrolysis of heavy water, no clusters
could be seen with normal devices. However, the author succeeded in observing such special
clusters on the back side of a thin palladium foil or on nuclear emulsions(4, 5). In 1994, further­
more, the author observed extraordinary sparks which were shining on the surface of a thin wire
electrode of cadmium during underwater discharge experiments(6). The sparks were found to be
really the itonic clusters and showed very curious behavior like Ball Lightning(BL) which was
observed in the natural environment. Therefore, the itonic cluster was called micro BL, which
could be easily generated on a pinched electrode by using Underwater Spark Discharge(USD)
mehtod(7). The itonic cluster was assumed to have interconnected electrons, which made the
cluster stable and negatively charged. The cluster could be derived through a leading wire by an
electrical potential(3).
          This paper describes several USD experiments to confirm the transport of micro BL by
the potential. Here micro BL was generated on a tip of two pinched anodes: a thin palladium
wire and a thin copper plate like a knife. Micro BL and its traces which were left on the wires
and the plates during the transport were examined in detail. Remarkable results were obtained.

2. Transport Experiments
          Transport experiments of micro BL through metals were carried out with an electrical .
potential.
          We could have selected any of electrodes to generate micro BL, but an anode was used
in this experiment. This situation was convenient to make micro BL transport on the metal. Two

                                               - 1 -

                                              -282-
kinds of the anodes were used: a thin palladium wire(0.5 mm    <I>     and about U cm long) and a
thin copper plate with a tapered tip like a knife(0.2 mm thick, 4 - 5 mm wide, 10 cm long and
about 15 deg). A cathode was a thin platinum wire(0.3 mm <I>         and about 15 cm long). Figure 1
shows the experimental arrangement for the anode of the copper knife. Since the copper knife
was tightly covered with a cap of sulfated papers, the contamination by water drops were effec­
tively avoided. Solutions of ordinary water mixed with potassium carbonate and hydroxide, and
cesium carbonate(each solution was about 1.5 Mol/1) were used as electrolyte solutions. The effe­
ctive lengths of the electrodes those portions were inserted in the solution were about 3 - 5 mm
and 35 mm for the anode and the cathode, respectively. On the other hand,-for the palladium
wire anode, an upper tip of the wire was put into two small strips of copper(? mm x 20 mm, 0.2
mm thick), one of which was connected with a leading wire.
         Disharges were carried out mainly under a continuous DC mode(around 150 V and 0.3
A). The voltage was sufficiently over a critical value for the generation of a pinched region. One
experiment with the copper knife anode was performed under a pulse AC mode, in which the
pulse was made by an electrical switch (120 V, 50 Hz, 10 msec ON, 20 - 30 sec OFF, phase =0
deg(fixed)).
         The transport of micro BL was examined by catching it on copper plates. The plate of
the anode itself was used for the copper knife anode, but the two small copper strips contacting
the upper tip of the palladium wire was available for the wire anode. All of the copper plates
were polished with diamond pastes like mirrors, and washed in an alcholic solution with ultra­
sonic vibration. Micro BL and its traces on the copper plates were examined with an optical
microscope(OM) and a scanning electron microscope(SEM). The latter was associated with an
energy dispersive X-ray analyzer(EDX) to determe elements. The window of EDX was made of
a special material such that light elements until C could be analyzed.


3. Experimental Results
         Many remarkable results which were related to the generation and the transport of micro
 BL were obtained with both of the anodes. Results with the palladium wire are first described.
The generation and the transport of micro BL through the wire were confirmed, which were
associated with a related phenomenon of nuclear transmutation. First, large clusters with curious
shapes were caught on the surface of the palladium wire over the water level. Figure 2 shows
micro BL with a hole which was obtained with the cesium carbonate solution. The EDX analysis
confirmed that micro BL consisted of a cluster of Ce, C and O elements which were the consti­
tuents of the electrolyte solution.
         Secondly, the transport of micro BL through the palladium wire was confirmed. Many
traces caused by micro BL were recorded on the small copper strips which were contacted with
the upper tip of the palladium wire. Figure 3 shows one of the traces, which was recorded about
12 cm distant from the lower tip of the wire. Micro BL was scattered out due to the inuniformity
at the connecting point. The fact clearly indicated that micro BL was derived through the wire
by the potential.
         Thirdly, the nuclear transmutation was observed on a lower tip of the palladium wire,



                                               - 2 -

                                              -283-
those portion were inserted in the solution of potassium carbonate and was pinched during 3 min.
DC discharges with about 120 V and 0.1 A, as shown in Fig. 4. The EDX spectrum at a dip is
shown in Fig. 5, in which transmuted elements such as Al, Si, Ni and Cu appeared. They were
not impurities at all: because highly pure materials were used for the palladium wires, electrolytes
and water, no impurity elements were not observed with EDX for the materials before the dis­
charges. Furthermore, if the small amount of the impurities solved in the solution were gathered
by the potential, they should have appeared on another surface of the pinched wire. But no impu­
rity elements were observed with EDX. The nuclear transmutation was a related phenomenon to
the formation of the itonic clsuter(3).
         Next, other remarkable results were obtained during the USD experiments with the cop­
per knife electrode, which further confirmed the transport of micro BL. This was due to a fact
that copper was sensitive to the charges of micro BL. Fourthly, curious traces which were record­
ed on the copper knife by moving micro BL. Two different kinds of the traces were observed:
two parallel rails and one stroke writing. Figure 6 shows one of the typical traces for the former.
There were many similar traces which were highly concentrated in a lower portion of the knife.
Some of them were overlapped each other and eventually made the knife dark as the discharge
time became longer. In Fig. 6, a brown portion was observed between two rails, which might
have been caused by a direct touch with micro BL. The latter was mysterious traces like being
made by one stroke writing. It was very long, but with not so frequently appeared. While the
former was uniformly derived towards the positive potential, the latter suddenly changed its di­
rection. Similar traces as the latter were already observed in previous USO experiments(8). Some
reactions in micro BL to change its direction were suggested to occur. But the detailed mecha­
nisms of forming the latter was still unclear.
          Fifthly, micro BL with a slightly different shape was observed. One of the typical pro­
ducts is shown in Fig. 8, which was caught on the copper knife with the potassium carbonate so­
lution. Micro BL ha� a core in the central region, over which itonic clusters including the pota­
ssium carbonate electrolyte were coated. The surface clusters consisted of many small clusters.
Figure 9 shows a partial break up of such cluster, in which many surface clusters were left on the
copper plate during the tranport and a core became bare. Figure 10 shows a complete break up
of the cluster. While the cluster ran on the copper plate, many cluster debris were left through the
path. Here no core was found. The core might have gone away or the cluster might have no
core.
         Sixthly, remarkable products of thin tubes were obtained, which consisted of mainly a
carbon element. Figure 11 shows one of the tube products which was associated with an itonic
cluster. In previous experiments(9, 10), many similar tube products were already observed during
USD. But most of the tubes were separated from the clusters, because they should have emerged
with a high velocity. It was rare to find the tube associated with the cluster(ll).
          Seventhly, remarkable products of thin films were left on the copper plate and also con­
sisted of mainly a carbon element. There were many thin films which had various shapes due to
folding. Figure 12 shows one of the typical thin carbon films. This was generated just from the
collapse of micro BL which ran from left with a trace of two parallel rails. In the previous papers



                                               - 3 -

                                              -284-
(9, 10), many products of thin films were already observed during similar USO, but with differ­
ent materials of an electrode and electrolyte. Even when the different materials containing no
carbon element were used, the same element of carbon appeared as a constituent of the curious
tubes and films. More heavy elements such as iron were sometimes generated.
         Lastly, remarkable products of micro tornadoes were obtained. Many products with a
       r
long cu ling string of connecting debris were observed on the copper knife, as shown in Fig. 13.
This looked like an eruption with a high speed rotation. There were both of clock wise and anti­
clock wise rotations. Figure 14 was an important figure, which clearly indicated that the eruption
with the rotation was generated from micro BL. It ran from left recording a trace of two rails
and an eruption occurred in a central area. Here, the rotation was anti-clock wise so that an upper
product of the eruption was left behind but a lower one was pushed ahead, respectively. The
eruption could be called "micro tornado."

4. Discussion
         Experimental results related to the transport of micro Bl were obtained in the preceding
section. Several properties of micro BL were made clear.
          First, the generation of micro BL was reconfirmed by really catching it on the palladium
wires and the copper plates. There were two kinds of micro clusters: electrode clusters and ele­
ctrolyte ones. The number of the former was small in this experiment. This meant that the clust­
ering process on the anode was more moderate than on the anode(?). Anyway micro BL consist­
ed of an atomic cluster in the special state, as The Nattoh Model predicted. Since the electrons
were taken away during the transport, micro BL eventually broke up into a large number of de­
bris, those traces were left on the copper plates, as shown in Fig. 10.
          Secondly, the nuclear transmutation was also observed on the tip of the palladium wire.
Since various nuclear reactions in the itonic cluster could contribute to the nuclear transmutation,
the schemes of the trasmutation were not so simple. However, this phenomena were another evi­
dence for the formation of the itonic cluster.
          Thirdly, the transport of micro BL was cleaerly confirmed for both of the electrodes of
the palladium wire and the copper knife. For the former, traces caused by micro BL were found
about 12 cm distant from the lower tip of the anode. This was an obvious evidence for the fact
that micro BL moved through the wire under the potential. The other evidence was traces with
two pallarel rails, which were recorded on the copper plates by moving micro BL. The plates
were so sensitive to the charges of micro BL that its transport was clearly recorded.
         Fourthly, the generation of thin carbon films and tubes were reconfirmed in this experi­
ment as well. Many products of carbon films and tubes were already observed in the previous
experiments(9, 10), and it was found that they emerged from the clsuter. In this experiment,
however, individual micro BL was taken out from the birth point and was made to release the
similar products of films and tubes. This was an additional evidence for the generation of the thin
carbon films and tubes from micro BL.
        Fifthly, the mechanisms of those remarkable results could be well explained by The
Nattoh Model(3). As the model predicted, micro BL was the special cluster with the negative



                                                - 4 -

                                               -285-
charges and was strongly compressed to induce the Electro-Nuclear Collapse(ENC) reactions.
Here it would be reasonable to consider that a tiny black hole was formed instantaneously. There
were two branches of completely broken materials: an isotropical explosion with a thin spherical
film and a release of a thin tube via a white hole. It would be extremely surprising that the con­
ventional elements such as carbon and iron were regenerated by ENC(called Electro-Nuclear Re­
generation(ENG)). A hypothetical mechanisms for ENG was proposed elsewhere(3).
         Lastly, it should be noticed that ENRs could easily take place in the natural environment.
Natural phenomena related to ENRs have been searched so far and evidence during earthquakes
were already obtained by the author(l2). Micro tornado which was observed in this experiment
was very suggestive to an origin of natural tornadoes. The natural tornadoes have the rotation of
an extremely high speed which could not be explained by the conventional hydrodynamics. Here
we could propose a promising hypothesis for the origin of the natural tornadoes: first, discharges
with a very high current took place in a cloudy atmosphere, and secondly, BL with a large di­
mension are generated and lastly, eruptions with a high speed rotation could be induced. We
could expect that the mechanisms of the natural tornado could be studied in detail by examining
micro tornado which can be easily generatged in laboratory.
